---

## Decisions

Where the spec is silent or ambiguous, the PO chose the simplest party-game behaviour:

| ID | Topic | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D-01 | "all 25 cells" (spec, Board → End Game) | Board size is dynamic, so End Game is auto-offered when **all cells** (N × number of point values) are used. "25" was the 5×5 default example only. |
| D-02 | Unique names | Compared after trimming, case-insensitive. Players and teams follow the same rules. |
| D-03 | Timer at 0 | Stops at 0 and shows "Time's up!"; nothing is auto-scored or auto-closed. The host can still score and must click "Back to Board". **Amended 2026-10-06:** the beep at 0 is removed (owner found it annoying); the timer is silent. |
| D-04 | Refresh with question open | Question reopens with scoring done so far preserved; timer restarts at full length. |
| D-05 | Manual ± adjust step | Equals the smallest configured point value (dynamic, no hard-coded 100). Manual adjusts never change cell ownership/color. |
| D-06 | Opened cell | Once opened, a cell is always marked used on "Back to Board" (grey if no Correct). No "cancel" of a question. Scoring marks inside a question cannot be undone; use the manual ± buttons. |
| D-07 | Mode switch with too many names | Entries are not deleted automatically; Start is blocked with a message until the host removes extras. |
| D-08 | Wrong after Correct | Allowed for contestants not yet scored on that question (spec only disables other Correct buttons). |
| D-09 | End Game early | Requires a confirm dialog; unused cells are ignored. |
| D-10 | Ties | All contestants sharing the top score (including all at 0 or all negative) are co-winners. |
| D-11 | Not enough playable categories | Setup shows a message with a link to Admin; Start disabled. **Amended 2026-10-11 (D-32):** this now applies only when no category is playable; with fewer than N playable the host simply starts a smaller board. |
| D-12 | Admin changes during a game | A running game uses the board loaded at Start (stored in local state); admin edits apply to the next game. |
| D-13 | Point value validation | Positive integer 1–1,000,000; duplicates → 409. |
| D-14 | Category name | Non-empty after trimming, max 100 characters, unique case-insensitive (duplicate → 409). |
| D-15 | New Game | Clears all saved state including names; settings are reloaded from the API. |
| D-16 | Contestants mid-game | Adding/removing contestants after Start is not supported. |
| D-17 | Target database / runtime | The target database is **MariaDB 10.4** (MySQL 8 is no longer a target; changed 2026-10-04). The reference environment is XAMPP (MariaDB 10.4 + PHP 8.0, managed via phpMyAdmin). All SQL and PHP must stay compatible with **MariaDB 10.4** and **PHP 8.0** (no PHP 8.1+ features; no SQL syntax or collations missing on MariaDB 10.4). Schema must be importable via phpMyAdmin as well as the `mysql` CLI. **Amended 2026-10-04 (D-20):** code must also run on the newer managed MariaDB version Hostinger provides — use standard SQL only (no version-specific syntax, functions or collations; utf8mb4 with a collation available on both). |
| D-18 | Admin deletes | Every admin delete (point value, category, question) asks for a plain-text confirm first; Cancel sends nothing. A confirmed delete of a used point value still reaches the API and shows the 409 inline (US-19). Admin conveniences that only prefill forms (e.g. clickable coverage cells) are allowed. Added 2026-10-04 (Sprint 2). **Amended 2026-10-11 (D-31):** deleting a point value no longer returns 409 and the coverage grid is removed; the confirm-before-delete rule still applies. **Re-amended 2026-10-11 (D-31 amended):** the point-value 409 is back (a used value cannot be deleted); the coverage grid stays removed. |
| D-19 | Admin layout | The admin page targets desktop/laptop widths (≥ 1024 px). Narrower widths may scroll tables horizontally inside their panel; mobile layout is out of scope. Messages and long unbroken names must not make the whole page scroll horizontally at ≥ 1024 px (follow-up for Sprint 2 O-5, fix with the Sprint 4 CSS work). Added 2026-10-04 (Sprint 2). |
| D-20 | Admin protection / hosting | Stakeholder decision 2026-10-04, **reverses** the spec rule "Admin page is not password-protected". BrainRush is deployed to Hostinger shared hosting as a public staging site and redeployed after each sprint. Admin writes (POST/PUT/DELETE on `api/*.php`) need HTTP Basic Auth when credentials (username + password hash) are set in `api/config.php`; with none set, writes are loopback-only (403 otherwise), so the public site fails closed. GET stays open; answers readable via the API is **accepted** as a known party-game limitation. Hosted site must use HTTPS. Details in US-29; US-28 README criterion amended accordingly. **Amended 2026-10-04 (US-29 acceptance):** "local" means loopback address **and** local `Host` (`localhost` / `127.0.0.1` / `[::1]`) **and** no forwarding headers; anything else (custom local host names, reverse proxies) needs admin keys. Half-configured or non-hash admin keys fail closed with 500 + hint. |
| D-21 | "Quit to setup" on the board | Interim Sprint 3 escape only. In Sprint 4 it is **removed** once End Game (US-10, confirm per D-09) and New Game (US-16) exist: the single way out of a game is End Game → Results → New Game, so a host cannot throw a game away without seeing the results. Added 2026-10-05 (Sprint 3). |
| D-22 | Contestant name length | Max **30 characters** after trimming, the same for players and teams. Enforced by the setup input (`maxlength`). A restored saved game with a longer name counts as invalid (Setup is shown, see US-17 corrupt-data rule). Added 2026-10-05 (Sprint 3). |
| D-23 | When Start is disabled | Start is disabled outright, with a visible reason, only for blocks the host has to fix structurally: too many entries for the mode (D-07), not enough playable categories (D-11), setup data failed to load (retry offered), and while the Start request is running. Field errors (names, category count, timer) are checked on click, all shown at once inline, and focus moves to the first problem. Added 2026-10-05 (Sprint 3). |
| D-24 | Setup form on refresh | Not saved. Refreshing during setup shows the defaults again (Players, one empty entry, timer from settings, current playable categories). Only a started game is saved (US-17). Added 2026-10-05 (Sprint 3). |
| D-25 | Revealed answer on refresh | Once "Show Answer" was clicked, the answer stays shown after a refresh mid-question (the room has already seen it). Extends D-04. Added 2026-10-05 (Sprint 4). |
| D-26 | End Game on a full board | The D-09 confirm applies only while unused cells remain. When every cell is used, both End Game buttons (board bar and the "all played" offer) go straight to Results. New Game on Results needs no confirm. Added 2026-10-05 (Sprint 4). |
| D-27 | Ranks on Results | Equal scores share a rank (standard competition ranking: 1, 1, 3). Equal scores are listed in setup order. Winner/tie text follows D-10. Added 2026-10-05 (Sprint 4). |
| D-28 | Saved-game versions | The saved game carries a `version`. A Sprint 3 (v1) save on the board is upgraded and restored; any other older or unknown version is discarded (Setup shown, US-17 corrupt-data rule). Any future change to the saved shape must bump the version and either migrate or discard safely. Added 2026-10-05 (Sprint 4). |
| D-29 | Beep after refresh | Browsers block audio until the user interacts with the page. After a refresh mid-question with no click or key press, the beep at 0 may be silent. The visible "Time's up!" is the guaranteed signal. Accepted as a known limitation and documented in the README; no "enable sound" prompt. Added 2026-10-05 (Sprint 4). **Obsolete 2026-10-06:** the beep was removed (see D-03). |
| D-30 | Game screen layout | Targets laptop 1366x768 and projector 1920x1080. Never a horizontal scroll. Vertical scroll is acceptable on Setup (a form), for unusually long question/answer text, and while the "all played" offer is shown. Board + scoreboard should fit one 1366x768 screen up to 6 rows and 6 contestants (met in Sprint 5, FU-03). Added 2026-10-05 (Sprint 4). **Amended 2026-10-05 (Sprint 5):** sizes mean the browser viewport (inner window). A windowed browser with tabs and an address bar has less height and may scroll a little; that is accepted, as are boards with more than 6 rows. Results with up to 6 contestants should also fit 1366x768 (met by FU-08). Vertical scroll there is accepted when very long names make the tie headline or the rows wrap. **Amended 2026-10-06 (iOS):** phones are supported as a best effort. Up to 600px wide the game page uses a compact layout (no horizontal scroll; a 6 × 6 board with 6 players fits a 375x812 phone with little or no scrolling). A phone held sideways scrolls vertically. Hover styles apply only to devices with a real pointer, and form fields stay at 16px or more on touch screens (no iOS zoom). The admin page stays desktop-only (D-19). |
| D-31 | Question bank (replaces "one question per slot") | Owner decision 2026-10-11, **replaces** the spec rule "exactly one question per category + point value slot". Questions belong only to a category and have no point value; a category is a pool. Playable = at least as many questions as there are point values (and at least one). On each new game the server draws, per chosen category, as many random, distinct questions as there are point values and places them on the rows in random order. Pure random, no play history. An exact duplicate question text in the same category (case-insensitive) → 409 "This category already has this question". Deleting a point value is no longer blocked by questions. The admin coverage grid is removed (US-22 retired); the categories table shows "M questions (needs P)" / "X more needed". The board payload shape is unchanged and D-12 still holds: the board drawn at Start is kept in local state, so a refresh does not redraw. Accepted consequence: the point value no longer reflects a question's difficulty, so bank questions are written at a similar, moderate level. Details in US-30; US-19, US-20, US-21, US-24, US-25 and D-18 amended. **Amended 2026-10-11 (Sprint 6 acceptance):** (a) a `points` field sent with a question is ignored, not rejected; (b) a short category shows "Not playable: X more needed"; (c) the duplicate check is accent-insensitive as well as case-insensitive (DB collation, same as category names), and it compares the first 191 characters only. Both are accepted, since questions are normally far shorter (OBS-2, OBS-3); (d) bank Science #5 is reworded to "Which vitamin does the human body make when exposed to ultraviolet rays?" (Vitamin D), so it no longer hints at Science #7 "The skin" or #16 "The Sun" (OBS-5). The change is applied in `docs/question-bank.md`, both schema files, the migration and the local DB. **D-31 amended 2026-10-11 (owner, after reviewing Sprint 6: "show the question score value in the questions list"; asked to clarify, the owner chose to bring back a point value on each question).** This replaces the "no point value" parts above:
| D-32 | Number of categories per game | Owner decision 2026-10-11, **replaces** "pick exactly N". `categories_per_game` (N) is the **maximum**: the host picks at least 1 and at most N playable categories, and the board has that many columns (in tick order). Once N are ticked, the other boxes are disabled. The setting keeps its name and 1–10 range; the admin label reads "Max categories per game". Start is blocked for categories only when none is playable (D-11 amended). The server board endpoint is unchanged (it never enforced N). US-04 amended. |
- Each question has a required point value that must exist in `point_values`. A category may have **any number of questions per point value**: a bank per (category, point value), not one question per slot (`uq_slot` stays removed).
- On each new game, every board cell (category x point value) gets one random question from that category's questions with that point value. Pure random, no history. The payload shape is unchanged and D-12 holds.
- Playable = at least one question for every point value (the pre-Sprint 6 rule).
- Deleting a point value still used by questions → 409 again.
- Admin:
  - The question form has a points dropdown listing all point values, with no free-slot filtering.
  - The questions list has a Points column, sorted by category, then points, then id.
  - The categories table shows the question count and "Not playable: missing V1, V2".
  - The point values panel shows per-value question counts again.
  - The coverage grid stays removed (US-22 stays retired). The owner disliked the slot UI, and the categories status plus the per-value counts cover its purpose.
- Duplicate text in the same category → 409, unchanged and regardless of points. Items (c) and (d) above still apply.
- (a) is superseded: `points` is required and validated, not ignored. (b) is superseded by "missing V".
- The difficulty consequence above no longer applies: bank questions carry points ranked by difficulty within their category (`docs/question-bank.md`, 4 per value per category).
- The migration converts the pre-Sprint 6 slot schema and keeps `points`.
- The local DB, already converted by the first Sprint 6 migration (no `points`), is not a supported source. Restore it from the pre-Sprint 6 backup, then run the new migration (FU-13).

Details: US-30 (rewritten), with US-19, US-20, US-21, US-24, US-25 and D-18 re-amended. |
| D-33 | "Surprise me!" | Owner decision 2026-10-22. One tile in the category list with two behaviours: ticked alone it picks **3** random playable categories (capped by N and by the number of playable categories); ticked with other picks it takes one slot and becomes **1** random playable category not already picked. The draw happens in the browser at Start; the board API and the game log are unchanged and see real category ids. Randomly picked columns show "?" on the board until one of their questions is opened (saved with the game, so a refresh keeps it). US-31. |
| D-34 | Photo credit under picture questions | Owner decision 2026-10-22, for the CC BY / CC BY-SA photos of the Landmarks set. New column `questions.image_credit` (`VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, `''` = no caption; migration `sql/migrate-add-image-credit.sql`, before the code). The API returns it as `image_credit` (null when empty) in the admin list and the board payload and accepts it on create/update (optional, trimmed, max 255, one line of plain text, else 400). The question screen shows it as a small muted one-line caption "Photo: <credit>" right under the picture, as text, left-to-right in both languages; it takes the picture's bottom margin, so the picture size and the 1366x768 layout are unchanged. No caption for text questions or an empty credit (Flags). Admin: optional "Image credit" field under the image path; the list is unchanged. Format: `<author> / <licence>`, e.g. `Jane Doe / CC BY-SA 4.0`. |
| D-35 | Bigger photos in picture questions | Owner decision 2026-10-22, for the Landmarks photos. A photo (a picture that is not an `.svg`; flags stay as they are) gets a compact question layout: the language button sits on the prompt's line, the timer digits (2rem) and the answer text are smaller, the spacing is tighter, and the "Time's up!" line only takes room once time is up. The room goes to the photo: `clamp(7rem, calc(100vh - 25.2rem), 58.5vh)` on screens wider than 960px, about 340px tall at 1366x768 (flags 215px) and 632px at 1920x1080 (flags 528px); the owner first asked for bigger photos (280px), then for 20% more. A one- or two-line answer still fits with no page scroll; the two three-line answers (Big Ben, Muhammad Ali Mosque) scroll a little, as does a two-line answer once "Time's up!" shows, as D-30 accepts. The photo credit caption (D-34) is 0.6875rem (owner: one size smaller). |
| D-36 | Helpers (3 choices, Swap, Skip) | Owner decisions 2026-10-23. Every contestant gets 3 helpers per game, each usable once: **3 choices** (the answer plus 2 wrong options, A/B/C in random order), **Swap** (replace the open question) and **Skip** (lose half its value instead of risking all of it). (a) Wrong options are **stored per question** in English and Arabic (`questions.wrong_1`, `wrong_2`, `wrong_1_ar`, `wrong_2_ar`, `VARCHAR(255) NULL`; migration `sql/migrate-add-wrong-options.sql`, before the code) and written by the question-producer (`sql/add-wrong-options.sql`, reviewed by the owner per set). The 3 choices chip is greyed out on a question without them. (b) Using a helper makes the question **that contestant's only**: the other scoring rows are disabled, and only that contestant can use more helpers on it. (c) Helpers are allowed only before anyone is scored on the question and before the answer is shown; each use is confirmed first (no undo, D-06); the ± buttons do not restore them. (d) **Swap** draws from the **whole bank**: a random playable category, a random enabled point value, never a question on the current board, unseen first, otherwise the one seen longest ago (`POST questions.php?random=1`). It is scored at the new question's value, and the original cell closes with the new question's result. (e) **Skip** subtracts `Math.round(points / 2)` (at the swapped value after a Swap) and **closes the question for everyone**: the cell turns grey. (f) **Amended 2026-10-23 (fix loop 1):** every choice is shown cut at its first bracket: English at " (", Arabic at "(" with or without a space. This covers the correct answer ("Three (triplets)" → "Three"; "(accept …)" notes go too) and also the wrong options, because some Arabic wrong options carry an English gloss like their answers. So no option stands out by its brackets. The full answer still shows under Show Answer. The API rejects an option that equals the answer, typed or cut. The saved game goes to version 3; version 2 saves are upgraded with all helpers available (D-28). (g) Layout (**amended 2026-10-23, fix loop 1**):
- **Chips:** icon-sized chips (ABC, ⇄, ½) on the scoring rows and 3 small symbols on each scoreboard card.
- **Scoring rows:** the columns stay 1 : 1 as before. A row fits on one line at 1366x768 with 6 contestants (D-30) through compact rows: 0.92rem Correct/Wrong buttons, 1.5rem chips, tighter gaps.
- **Text question with 3 choices:** a compact layout like D-35's photo one. The question font is smaller, the choice rows are tight, choices of up to 24 characters sit side by side, the timer digits are 2rem, "Time's up!" takes room only once time is up, and the answer is smaller. In the 761-question sweep at 1366x768, 4 English views with the answer shown scroll: 1 long Egyptians question by 2 px, and 3 Landmarks photos with long answers, which D-35 already lists. No Arabic view scrolls.
- **Picture question with 3 choices:** the choices sit side by side, and the picture gives up their height.

US-32. |
| D-37 | Streak Challenge and leaderboard | Owner decisions 2026-10-23. (a) A solo mode, offered only in Players mode with exactly 1 player; it needs a challenger name (1–30 characters). *(Amended 2026-10-23 (owner): one "Challenger name" field replaces first + last name.)* (b) Questions are fully random from every category at an enabled point value, unseen first; the category picker is ignored, which keeps the leaderboard fair. (c) **3 multiple-choice answers**, so there is no self-judging. They reuse the helper's stored wrong options and its "cut at the first bracket" rule (D-36); a question without a complete pair borrows 2 answers from the same category and kind (picture or text), then from any category of that kind, and is skipped if it still has fewer than 2. The server shuffles the choices. (d) A wrong answer **or time's up** ends the run; "End run" ends it early. Timer = the setup timer; no Pause or Reset, and a refresh keeps the remaining time (saved deadline), so neither can be used to cheat. The helpers are not offered. (e) The leaderboard is **shared on the server** (`streaks` table, `sql/migrate-add-streak.sql` before the code): every run of 1 or more is stored; the top 10 shows the best run per person (challenger name, case-insensitive), the earlier run first on a tie, as "Name · N questions · 18 Oct 2026" (server date of the run). (f) Admin can delete runs (moderation of junk or offensive names, D-18 confirm). (g) **Accepted limitation:** the answers are in the payload and the POST is public, so a determined user could fake a score (same as the game log and the public board API). (h) The run is saved in its own browser key (`brainrush.streak`), so the board game's saved state (D-28) is unchanged. (i) **Added 2026-10-23 (PO acceptance):** a person is matched by the DB collation, so the match is accent-insensitive as well as case-insensitive ("José Fahim" = "Jose Fahim"), as for category names and duplicate questions (D-31 c). Accepted. The screen's own-row highlight compares lowercase text only, so an accent variant may not be highlighted. A player ranked below the top 10 gets the rank line but no highlighted row. (j) The leaderboard row reads "1. Ahmed Fahim · 13 questions · 18 Oct 2026" as text (screen readers). On screen the name sits left and "· 13 questions · 18 Oct 2026" right, which meets US-34. (k) The best-run query uses `ROW_NUMBER() OVER` (standard SQL window function, MariaDB 10.2+), allowed under D-17 once the hosted version is confirmed (FU-17). US-33, US-34. |
| D-38 | Player guide | Owner decision 2026-10-23. A separate static page `how-to-play.html` (no JS), in English only, with its own `css/guide.css` on top of the shared stylesheet. The game header links to it in a new tab, so a running game and an open question's timer are not touched. It names no category and no admin-set limit or default. It describes the game without helpers; a helpers section follows once that feature lands. US-35. |
| D-39 | Terms of Service | Owner decision 2026-10-23. A separate static page `terms.html`, in English only, with no JS and no CSS of its own (it reuses the guide styles). It is linked as "Terms" in the game header (new tab) and in the guide's header. Players don't accept anything: there is no checkbox, and Start is not affected. It names the owner, Ahmed Fahim, with the contact ahmedfahim89@gmail.com. A short "Your data" section replaces a separate privacy page. There is no governing-law clause. US-36. |
| D-40 | Double score question | Owner decision 2026-10-25. At Start the browser picks one random cell of the board (`game.double`, "<categoryId>:<points>", saved with the game; the saved version stays 3 and a save without it simply has no double, like `helpersOff`). It stays secret until opened; a popup then announces it and holds the timer. Only a Correct is doubled (`2 × p`); a Wrong costs `p`. The double belongs to the cell, so after a Swap the new question's points are doubled; Skip stays `Math.round(p / 2)`. The ± corrections are unchanged. Client-only: no API, DB or game-log change. US-37. |
| D-41 | Ads | Owner decision 2026-10-25. Google AdSense, set up for H5 Games Ads (AdMob is for native apps only). For now only a responsive display banner on the Streak leaderboard screen (the Results banner was dropped the same day so that the consent message never covers the winner announcement); interstitial/rewarded `adBreak` placements need a separate H5 Games Ads approval and can reuse the loader later. Off by default: the `ads_enabled`, `ads_client` and `ads_slot` settings (no migration; a missing row means off) are set in Admin. Maintenance mode also turns ads off. Test mode on localhost. A separate `privacy.html` now holds the Terms' "Your data" section (amends D-39) with an "Ads and cookies" section. Consent for the EEA/UK/CH uses AdSense's own Privacy & messaging, so there is no consent code. ads.txt goes on the root domain (`integration.ninja`), not in this repo, because Google reads it from the root domain. US-38. |
| D-42 | Search and sharing | Owner decision 2026-10-25. No hashtags or `meta keywords` (Google ignores both). Instead: descriptive titles and meta descriptions, canonical URLs (the game's is `/`, not `/index.html`), one visible intro line on the setup screen, Open Graph/Twitter tags with a 1200×630 `img/og-image.png`, a static JSON-LD block (`WebSite` + `WebApplication`) on the game page, `noindex` on admin, `robots.txt` (blocks `/api/` only, so crawlers can read admin's `noindex`) and a hand-kept `sitemap.xml`. Google Search Console is set up by the owner. US-39. |
| D-43 | Android app | Owner decisions 2026-10-26. BrainRush ships on Android as a **Trusted Web Activity** (TWA): a thin wrapper that opens the live site full screen in Chrome, so every web deploy updates the app and there is one codebase (Capacitor and native Kotlin were considered and not chosen). Package name **`ninja.integration.brainrush`** (permanent after the first Play upload). **No ads in the app at launch**: AdSense stays on the website only, and the Play listing says "Contains ads: No". The wrapper project lives in the **`android/`** subfolder of this repository, blocked on the web by `.htaccess` (Git deploy copies it); signing keys stay outside the repository and are git-ignored with the build output. The app is recognised by `?source=twa` (the manifest's start URL) or the `android-app://ninja.integration.brainrush` referrer, remembered per tab in `sessionStorage`. The first release adds four phone polish items: touch targets of at least 48dp, an offline page (a service worker that caches only that page, never `api/`), the Admin link hidden in the app, and Android Back handling. **Amended 2026-10-26 (owner, fix loop 1):** (a) Back on an open board question asks before closing it (a Back press is easily made by mistake, D-06); (b) in the app, links to the site's own pages open in the same window instead of a new tab, so app mode (no ads, no Admin) carries over. For this the guide, Terms and Privacy pages load one small shared script, `js/app.js` (amends D-38 and D-39 "no JS": on the website it changes nothing). (c) Accepted risk: anyone can add `?source=twa` to a website URL to hide the ads and the Admin link; it only affects ad revenue, since admin access is protected by the server (D-20). (d) Known limitation: Back before the first tap on a game restored at app start leaves the app (Chrome only lets a page add history after a user tap); the game stays saved. (e) The app hides the "New game" heading and the home intro (D-42 text for website visitors and search engines). US-40. |
| D-44 | About and Contact | Owner decision 2026-10-28, after Google's AdSense review checklist asked for About Us and Contact Us pages. Two static pages in the guide style, `about.html` and `contact.html`, in English only. Contact is by email only (the address already public in the Terms and Privacy); no contact form, so nothing new is stored. A shared footer (How to play, About, Contact, Privacy, Terms) on every public page; not on admin or the offline page. The Admin header link stays. US-41. |
| D-45 | Report a question (US-42) | Owner decision 2026-11-02 (FU-20). The Report form offers six reasons: wrong answer, typo, translation, outdated, picture problem (for the Flags and Landmarks photos) and other, plus an optional note of up to 200 characters. There is **no rate limit**: the API stores no IP, device id or hash of either, and the admin dismisses junk reports in the Reports panel. A report row holds only the question id, reason, note, status and time.
| D-46 | Daily Challenge day and size (US-43) | Owner decision 2026-11-02 (FU-21). The Daily Challenge changes at **midnight UTC**: the challenge date, the number #N and the countdown all use the UTC date, whatever the server or the player's time zone. Each challenge has **3 questions**, so the result reads "BrainRush Daily #N: 2/3" with 3 squares. The count and the time zone are set in one place in the code. For consistency the weekly Streak tab (US-45) also runs Monday to Sunday in UTC. |
| D-47 | Daily Challenge, share and weekly tab details (US-43, US-46, US-45) | PO decisions 2026-11-02 at the Sprint 8 acceptance; D-46 is unchanged. (a) **#N** = days since the first day this database served a challenge, plus 1, so local and live each start at #1. Accepted for the launch. Known risk: if every question of that first day is later deleted, the numbers shift. FU-30 pins #1 to the live launch date. *(Amended 2026-11-11 by FU-30, D-62 b: #N now counts UTC days from the constant `DAILY_LAUNCH_DATE` = 2026-11-02, plus 1, with a floor of #1; it no longer reads the DB, so local and live give the same #N for a date.)* (b) A question deleted from today's set is replaced on the next request, so later players may get a different question at that position. Accepted (an admin action, rare). (c) The leaderboard **opens on "All time"** as before; "This week" is one click away, and the rank line after a run already shows both ranks (US-45). (d) **US-46 "a device with a share sheet"** means a touch device (phone, tablet, the Android app) with the Web Share API, as US-43 says ("the phone's share sheet"). Desktop browsers always copy the text and show "Copied", even when they have a share dialog of their own: a host at a laptop pastes into a chat. Closing the sheet shows nothing. (e) The Daily Challenge uses the **setup timer**, as US-43 says. Results played with different timers are compared as they are (accepted). The Daily button shows in any mode and with any number of players. It is hidden in maintenance mode and during the Streak setup. (f) **Leaving a run:** in the app, Android Back on an open Daily question asks first, then ends the run, and the questions not answered score 0 (as D-43 a). The website has no Leave control: a run is at most 3 timers long, and a run left over from an earlier day closes by itself after midnight UTC (BUG-8-03 fix). (g) Side effects, all accepted: Daily questions go into the device's played list, so later games avoid them; Daily runs are not written to the game log; a run that crosses midnight UTC counts for the day it started; a question without stored wrong options is picked only when none with them is left, and its borrowed choices can then differ between players; the result needs the server, so it is not shown offline; Admin's Streak list shows the time as the DB stores it, while the public board shows the UTC date (the BUG-8-02 fix). |
| D-48 | Final round wager limit (US-44) | Owner decision 2026-11-02 (FU-22), TV-show style. A contestant may wager from 0 up to **their score or the largest enabled point value, whichever is higher**. So a contestant at 0 or below (or with a score below the largest value) can still wager up to the largest point value, e.g. 600, and get back in the game; a leader can wager their whole score. The limit is read from the enabled point values, never hard-coded. |
| D-49 | Final round and read-aloud details (US-44, US-48) | PO decisions 2026-11-02 at the Sprint 9 acceptance; D-48 is unchanged. (a) **The setting is taken at Start.** `final_round_enabled` is read again when Start Game is clicked and saved with the game, like `helpers_enabled`. "The setting is on" in US-44 means on for this game. A change in Admin affects new games only, as the Admin hint says. (b) **The largest enabled point value** is the largest value on this game's board, which holds the enabled values at Start. It doesn't change during a running game. (c) **The question is drawn at "Play final round"**, before the wagers, so its category can be shown first. The question text stays off the screen until "Show the question". (d) **Any number of contestants can be Correct** on the final question, because everyone answers (for example on paper). Each contestant is marked once, and the amount shows only after the mark. D-08's one-Correct rule stays for board questions. (e) **The round can't be stopped half-way on the website.** Once the question is open, See results waits until everyone is marked, and a contestant with no answer is marked Wrong. In the app, Android Back offers to stop the round after a confirm (D-43 a). This is the same reasoning as D-47 f. (f) **Wager entry:** one contestant at a time, in a masked field with Show. A locked wager can't be changed. Accepted limitations, at the same trust level as D-37 g: the dots show how many digits were typed, and the wagers and the drawn question are in the browser's saved game, where DevTools can read them. (g) **A refresh** on the final question restarts its timer at full length (D-04). The wagers, marks, shown answer and language are kept. (h) **No helpers and no double score** on the final question. Pause, Reset, Show Answer, the language button, Report (US-42) and the speaker (US-48) work as on a board question. (i) Final rounds are not written to the game log, which records started games only. (j) **"Read questions automatically" is a choice stored on the device** (`brainrush.readAloud`). It sits on the setup screen, is off by default and is not an Admin setting, because whether reading helps depends on the device's voices and the room. (k) Accepted US-48 limitations: (1) after a refresh, the browser may block automatic reading until the first click on the page; (2) the speaker is hidden for a language that has no voice on the device (Arabic on many Windows PCs, as US-48 says); (3) if a language has only an online voice, that voice is used (noted in `privacy.html`); (4) choice letters are read as "A: ..." in both languages. US-44, US-48. |
| D-50 | Final round name: "Golden Goal ⚽" (US-44) | Owner decision 2026-11-02. In everything players and hosts see (game, Admin, guide, Privacy, llms.txt), the final round is called **Shoot Your Shot** and the amount a contestant risks is their **bet** (not "wager"), e.g. "Shoot your shot?", "Sara, what's your bet?", "Bet locked". The rules (D-48, D-49) are unchanged. A proposed change to the scoring (first correct answer takes it and ends the game) was cancelled by the owner the same day. Code names and story/decision text may keep "final round" and "wager". **Amended 2026-11-03 (owner):** the name is now **Golden Goal ⚽** (football's sudden-death tiebreaker, matching D-51), with "Go for goal" / "Skip to results" on the offer; "Shoot Your Shot" was used for one day and never released. The ⚽ is decorative and hidden from screen readers. The amount is still a **bet**. The rules are amended by D-51. |
| D-51 | Golden Goal is a tiebreaker (US-44) | Owner decisions 2026-11-03, after the PO showed that a leader gains nothing from the round (with A on 500 and B on 300, A has no safe bet under D-48). (a) At the end of the game (End Game after D-09, or a full board, D-26), Golden Goal is offered **only when 2 or more contestants share the top score** (any value, including 0 or below); otherwise the game goes straight to Results. (b) **Everyone** bets and is marked, not only the tied contestants. (c) Bets, the D-48 limit and D-49 stay as they are. (d) The host still chooses "Go for goal" or "Skip to results"; Skip shows the tie (D-10). (e) If 2 or more are still level at the top after everyone is marked, the host is offered another round, with a new question never used in this game and new bets from the current scores, until the tie is broken or the host skips; if no question is left, Results show the tie. (f) No "Golden Goal is on" notice on the board and no "safe bet" hint. |
| D-52 | Golden Goal details (US-44, D-51) | PO decisions 2026-11-03 at the Sprint 9 re-acceptance. D-48, D-50 and D-51 are unchanged. D-49 still applies to every round, with its button names read as the D-50 names. (a) **Android Back on a repeat offer** (round 2 or later) goes to Results with the tie and no confirm, as Skip does. The scores have changed since the board, so going back to the board makes no sense. Back on the first offer still returns to the board. (b) **Android Back on a question where everyone is marked and the top is still tied** goes straight to Results with the tie and no confirm, which skips the next round. Accepted for this sprint, because the result shown (a tie) is one the host could also reach with Skip. FU-34 adds a confirm, because a Back press is easily made by mistake (D-43 a). (c) **The button after a round that leaves the top still tied** should read **"Next round"**, because "Continue" can be read as "continue to the results". "Continue" is accepted for this sprint, and FU-34 renames it. Wording only, no rule change. (d) **US-44's story sentence** now states the tiebreaker purpose. The title stays "Final round with wagers", with the player-facing name in brackets. (e) **"A question never used in this game"** (D-51 e) is met for the board's questions and every earlier Golden Goal question, which are excluded from the draw. A question brought in by the Swap helper is not excluded; it is only avoided through the played list (unseen first), so it can come back only when no unseen question is left. FU-35 excludes it too. (f) FU-33 items 1 and 3 lapsed with D-51: a 409 now ends the game on Results with the tie instead of showing the Swap wording, and a single contestant can never be tied, so the round is never offered to one. *(Amended 2026-11-11, FU-34 and FU-35, D-62 c, d: (b) in the app this Back now asks first, "Stop Golden Goal and show the results? The tie stands."; (c) the button reads "Next round"; (e) Swap-in questions are now also excluded from the Golden Goal draw.)* US-44. |
| D-53 | Kilo Meshakkal category (US-53) | Owner decision 2026-11-03. **Kilo Meshakkal** (كيلو مشكل, "a kilo of assorted") is a **built-in** category: players tick it in Setup like any other (sorted by name, shown when at least one real category is playable, counts toward `categories_per_game`), but it has no row in `categories`, so Admin does not list or edit it, and "Surprise me!" never picks it. On the board it is one column; each box holds a random question from **any** playable category at that box's value, from different categories where possible, never a question already on the board, unseen first (US-30). The boxes show an **emoji instead of the value**, and the column holds each enabled value once in a **random order**, not ascending (owner request). The value shows when the box is opened ("Kilo Meshakkal: Football – 300") and on a used box; screen readers hear "Kilo Meshakkal, mystery value". A refresh restores the same emoji and order. Swap, Report, the double cell and Golden Goal work as on any box. The game log records it as "Kilo Meshakkal". |
| D-54 | Kilo Meshakkal details (US-53, D-53) | PO decisions 2026-11-03 at the Sprint 10 acceptance; D-53 is unchanged. (a) **Different categories come before unseen questions** inside the column: a box first takes a category not used yet in the column, then, among those, one with an unseen question (US-30). The values with the fewest unseen options are drawn first, so on a partly played bank a box takes a seen question only when no unused category has an unseen one at that value. (b) **Emoji list:** 12 faces (🎲 🔥 👀 🤯 🍿 💀 😭 ✨ 🍉 🧃 🎯 🎁), no Unicode 14 emoji, so they render on Windows 10. With more than 12 enabled values the faces repeat in a cycle; "a different emoji per box" holds up to 12 values. (c) **Never ascending:** a shuffle that comes out ascending is shuffled again. (d) **Swap on a mix box** shows "Swapped from Kilo Meshakkal – 200": the value is revealed once the box is open anyway. (e) A picked question deleted by the admin while the board is built gives **409** "The questions changed meanwhile. Please try again.", like the Swap draw. (f) **The name is reserved** (BUG-10-01): Admin cannot create or rename a real category to "Kilo Meshakkal" (case-insensitive), 409 "\"<name>\" is the name of a built-in category". (g) The name constant is repeated in `api/questions.php`, `api/game-log.php` and `api/categories.php`; FU-38 moves it to one place. US-53. |
| D-55 | Check value band (US-47) | PO decision 2026-11-03, before Sprint 11. (a) **Band from rank, no point values in code.** Sort the **enabled** point values ascending; n = their count, r = a value's rank (1 = smallest). Expected centre `c = 80 − 40 × (r − 1) / (n − 1)` (n = 1: `c = 60`), rounded to a whole number; band = `c − 20` to `c + 20`, kept within 0–100, ends included. So the smallest value expects 60–100% correct and the largest 20–60%, whatever the values are. Example, 5 enabled (100–500): 100 → 60–100%, 200 → 50–90%, 300 → 40–80%, 400 → 30–70%, 500 → 20–60%. Example, 3 enabled (e.g. 100, 300, 500): 60–100%, 40–80%, 20–60%. (6 enabled: 60–100, 52–92, 44–84, 36–76, 28–68, 20–60.) The numbers 80, 40, 20 and the attempt threshold 20 are named constants, not point values. (b) **Mark:** "Check value" shows when attempts ≥ 20 (`MIN_ATTEMPTS`) and the shown % correct (whole number, `round(100 × correct / attempts)`) is outside the band. It means "check it", not "move it": a smallest-value question can't be too easy, and a largest-value one below 20% may have a wrong answer or unclear text. The mark may have a tooltip such as "Expected 50–90% for 200". (c) **Disabled point value:** no rank, so **no mark**; attempts and % still show. The bands follow the enabled values at the time Admin is opened, so enabling or disabling a value recomputes them. (d) **Attempts and correct:** every outcome is an attempt (correct, wrong, nobody, timeout); only "correct" is correct. % correct = correct / (correct + wrong + nobody + timeout). (e) **Board outcome**, decided once when the question closes, from the marks at that moment: a contestant marked Correct → correct; else anyone marked Wrong → wrong; else the timer reached 0 → timeout; else → nobody (includes a Skip with no mark). A question swapped away by Swap is not counted; the new question is. Later ± corrections are not sent again. The double score (D-40) changes nothing. (f) **Streak:** a right choice → correct, a wrong choice → wrong, time's up → timeout. A question left open by "End run" is not counted. (g) **Pooled:** board and Streak add to the same counters, one set per question. Accepted: a board "correct" means anyone in the room knew it and Streak offers 3 choices, so both run higher than a solo free answer; the band's lenient width allows for it. (h) **Kilo Meshakkal** questions are real questions and count like any board question, at the question's own point value (the box's value). (i) **Not counted in this sprint:** Golden Goal questions (everyone answers, many can be correct) and Daily Challenge questions, as US-47 names only board and Streak. (j) Admin Questions shows a one-line hint with the band of each enabled value, e.g. "Check value: 20+ attempts and % correct outside 100: 60–100% · 200: 50–90% · …". US-47. |
| D-56 | Question difficulty stats details (US-47, D-55) | PO decisions 2026-11-03 at the Sprint 11 acceptance; D-55 is unchanged except for the wording in (b). (a) **No saved-game version bump.** `statsSent` and `current.timedOut` are optional fields (`STORAGE_VERSION` stays 4, `STREAK_VERSION` 1). A game or run saved before the deploy keeps going and sends no stats until a new one starts; a broken field discards the save to Setup (D-28). Accepted. (b) **One enabled value:** D-55 a's formula gives a centre of 60, so the band is 40–80%; "the smallest value expects 60–100%" holds from 2 enabled values. With none enabled, no question is marked and the hint says so. (c) **Board timer and refresh:** the board timer restarts after a refresh (D-04), so "the timer reached 0" is only seen while the page is open; it is saved, so a Reset or refresh after it keeps `timeout`. A question opened but never closed (tab closed, New Game elsewhere) is not counted. Accepted. (d) **Moving or editing a question keeps its counts:** they describe the question, not the value, so after a move the mark is checked against the new value's band. There is no reset in Admin; FU-43 adds one. (e) **Privacy:** no change. A stats row is an anonymous count per question, not data about a player, so the optional sentence suggested in the dev note is not needed. (f) The API's `sort=percent_correct` (task 4) stays although Admin sorts in the browser; questions with no attempts are always last. (g) `is_missing_table()` moved to `api/db.php`, shared by reports and question stats; no behaviour change. Accepted. (h) **Admin at 1024 px:** after the BUG-11-01 fix the Questions table fits with no scrollbar; its rows are tall (median 263 px, 287 px before the fix), which D-19 allows (only the page must not scroll). FU-44 (Could) improves it. (i) **Deploy guide:** step 8.4 lists only `index.html` and `admin.html`, but the new `style.*.css` hash also changes `how-to-play.html`, `about.html`, `contact.html`, `privacy.html` and `terms.html`. FU-40 gives the full list and FU-41 fixes the guide. US-47. |
| D-57 | Reset all stats (US-47 change request) | Owner request 2026-11-03: "in the same line of 'Check value: 20+ …' add a button to purge question_stats". PO decisions at the acceptance the same day (branch `cr-reset-stats`). (a) **API:** `DELETE api/question-stats.php` is admin only (`require_admin()` first, D-20): 401 without a login or with a wrong one, 403 from another computer when no login is configured, 200 `{"deleted": N}` (N = stats rows removed, i.e. questions that had attempts; 0 on a second reset), 503 when the table is missing. A prepared `DELETE FROM question_stats` (no TRUNCATE, no DDL right needed). The public POST is unchanged; other methods 405 with `Allow: POST, DELETE`. (b) **Admin:** a "Reset all stats" button at the right end of the "Check value" hint line, in the Delete button style. It is hidden while the stats are missing (table missing or an older API), disabled with the tooltip "Nothing to reset: no question has any attempts yet." when nothing is recorded, and disabled while a reset runs (one request per click). A confirm (D-18) says it cannot be undone; on OK the list reloads and "Stats reset: N questions cleared." shows until the next list change (reload, filter, sort, add, edit, delete; BUG-CR-01). An error stays until the next reset. (c) **No undo:** the confirm and the phpMyAdmin export (deploy step 2) are enough for a party game. If the reload after a successful reset fails, its error shows instead of the success line; accepted. (d) **Robustness:** a question without stats fields (the crash seen live while the new `admin.js` ran with an old `api/questions.php`) shows "-" with the migration note, as for a missing table. (e) No DB, game or Privacy change: nothing new is stored. (f) **FU-43 stays open, narrowed** to a reset of **one** question (after it is reworded or moved, or fake counts on one question), because a full reset also throws away every good count. A burst of fake counts across many questions is now handled by this button. US-47. |
| D-58 | Arabic interface strings, game terms and digits (US-49) | PO decisions 2026-11-09 at the Sprint 13 acceptance. (a) **AI-drafted Arabic accepted pending native review.** The 293 Arabic strings in `js/i18n.js` are AI-drafted Modern Standard Arabic. They are accepted for release; the owner or an Arabic speaker reviews them (FU-48). A change from the review is a table edit only, with no story or rule change. (b) **Game terms are translated**, not kept in English: Golden Goal = "الهدف الذهبي", Sudden Death Streak = "سلسلة الموت المفاجئ", the helpers = "ثلاثة خيارات" / "تبديل" / "تخطّي". The helper is named with the noun ("تخطّي"); the imperative "تخطَّ" is used only on the Golden Goal "Skip to results" button. The ⚽, the helper chips ABC / ⇄ / ½ and the A/B/C choice letters stay as symbols. The owner may switch any of these names back to English after the review; each one is a single table entry. (c) **Western digits (0-9)** in Arabic everywhere: board, scores, timer, bets, dates, Daily #N and share text, as on the board and in Admin. Bets typed in Arabic-Indic digits are still accepted. Signed numbers are isolated left to right, so "−200" never flips. US-49. |
| D-59 | What stays English, and the language choice (US-49) | PO decisions 2026-11-09 at the Sprint 13 acceptance. (a) **Server error texts** (the API's `error` field) stay English, also inside an Arabic sentence ("تعذّر بدء اللعبة: <server text>"); the API is not localized. Errors made in the browser (network, unexpected response, HTTP code) are translated. (b) **Category names** (including Kilo Meshakkal), player names, questions, answers, wrong options, the admin's "New questions" banner and photo credits are data and show as stored. No Arabic category names are added. *(Amended 2026-11-09 by D-61: category names, including Kilo Meshakkal, now have Arabic names.)* (c) **No auto-detect of the browser language.** English is the default. Arabic is used only after the player clicks "عربي"; the choice is saved on the device as `brainrush.lang` = `ar`, and clicking "EN" removes the key. (d) The guide, Terms, Privacy, About, Contact, Admin and `offline.html` stay English and left to right; `<title>`, meta, Open Graph and JSON-LD stay English (D-42). *(Amended 2026-11-11 by D-64 b: on the game page only, the `<title>`, h1 and footer brand follow the language, so Arabic shows "مخ رش" as the tab title; meta, Open Graph and JSON-LD stay English, and the other pages stay English.)* (e) The language is not stored in the saved game, so the saved-game versions are unchanged (D-28 does not apply). (f) **Accepted as they are:** the OK / Cancel buttons of confirms follow the browser's language; on the scoreboard card the + and − buttons are mirrored in Arabic (OBS-1); the Arabic share text carries invisible bidi marks around names and scores; switching language during a sub-second request shows the button's normal label until it ends; at 375 px the Arabic Setup is 22 px and the leaderboard up to 55 px taller than English (neither is a D-30 one-screen view; FU-50). US-49. |
| D-60 | New screens must be bilingual (US-49, US-50) | PO decision 2026-11-09. Sprint 13 ran before Sprint 12, so the US-50 custom category screens do not exist yet. From now on every new or changed visible string goes into both tables in `js/i18n.js` (English and Arabic), through `t()` / `tn()`, and each sprint's tests include Arabic and RTL for the screens it adds. For Sprint 12 this means the "Add my own category" form, its validation messages and the save, export and import messages; the same applies to the US-52 buzzer pages if that story is committed. New Arabic strings are AI-drafted under D-58 a until reviewed. US-49. |
| D-61 | Arabic category names (US-49 change request) | Owner request and decisions 2026-11-09; PO acceptance the same day. Amends D-59 b for category names only. (a) **Stored in the database**, in a new nullable `categories.name_ar` column (VARCHAR(100)), edited in Admin (an optional "Arabic name" field on the add form, and Rename edits both names). Not a table in the client, so a new category is translated without code. API: optional, trimmed, at most 100 characters, one line; empty or `null` clears it; a PUT without `name_ar` keeps the current one. (b) **Style:** the fun categories (Gen Z, AI, Egyptians, Food & Drink, Cartoons & Childhood, Who Am I?) in Gen Z / Egyptian youth style; the plain ones in Modern Standard Arabic. The names are AI-drafted and accepted pending the native review (FU-54). (c) **Kilo Meshakkal** has no DB row, so its Arabic name "كيلو مشكّل" lives in `js/i18n.js` (`cat.mixName`); English stays "Kilo Meshakkal". (d) **Setup tile order** stays English alphabetical (the API order) in both languages, so ticks and tiles don't move on a language switch; the Arabic list is therefore not alphabetical (accepted). (e) **Only the label changes:** ids, column order, Surprise me, game log and stats use ids as before. A category without an Arabic name, and a game, Streak run or Daily saved before the change, show the English name; saved-game versions are unchanged (the new fields are optional). (f) **Still English** (D-59 a, d): server error messages that name a category, and the Admin lists, reports and game logs (FU-56). *(Amended 2026-11-11 by FU-56: in Arabic the client puts the Arabic name of a known category into the English server sentence, and Admin Questions and Reports show the Arabic name next to the English one; game logs stay English, FU-58.)* (g) **Deploy order:** the migration runs before the code; there is no fallback for a missing column (FU-53). (h) Accepted: one extra small query per Swap / Golden Goal draw (OBS-C4); BUG-13-04 (Flags "أعلام") and "كرتون زمان" are left to the native review (FU-54) and can be changed in Admin without code. US-49. |
| D-62 | CR FU-62 / FU-30 / FU-34 / FU-35 details | PO decisions 2026-11-11 at the acceptance of CR `cr-fu62-30-34-35`. (a) **Category names (FU-62, amends D-54 f):** on create and rename the English name has every run of whitespace (Unicode-aware: tab, newline, no-break and ideographic spaces) collapsed to one space and is trimmed, and is stored that way; the built-in-name 409 and the duplicate-name 409 compare the collapsed name. A name made only of such spaces gives **400 "Name is required"** instead of being stored as an invisible name (accepted: an invisible category tile is worse). The Arabic name is not collapsed. The Admin success line echoes the typed name (cosmetic, accepted). (b) **Daily #N (FU-30, amends D-47 a):** counted from `DAILY_LAUNCH_DATE` = 2026-11-02 (UTC) in `api/daily.php`; consistent with live Daily #10 on 2026-11-11. A day before the launch date is #1. Local numbers before 2026-11-02 all read #1 (accepted). (c) **Golden Goal Back confirm (FU-34, amends D-52 b, c):** app mode only, as every Android Back rule (D-43 a); the website has no Back handling on this screen. Cancel stays and re-arms Back. (d) **Swap exclusion (FU-35, amends D-52 e):** a closed swapped cell saves the swapped-in id as the optional `used[key].swap.id` (no saved-game version bump, D-56 a pattern); a game saved before the deploy keeps going but its earlier Swap questions are not excluded (accepted, one game at most). The Swap helper's own draw still excludes only the board's questions (out of scope; FU-65). *(Amended 2026-11-11 by D-63 c: FU-65 and the real-device Back check FU-66 cancelled by the owner.)* |

With the columns shown the table scrolls sideways only below about 950 px (tester measurement; the FU-44 dev note said about 980). The toggle is still not remembered.
